# CONTRACT — `U-STOREROOM-JSON-CROSSING`: the crossing's value tree becomes the bare JSON tree, and every outside class is refused at the package's own boundary

**Status: GATE 3 GREEN — CONTRACT AUTHORED 2026-10-23; RED SET RUN 2026-10-23 (`23` PASS / `25` FAIL); GREEN RUN 2026-10-23 (`48` PASS / `0` FAIL).** Gate 1 closed `APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS`; the architect granted go-ahead on 2026-10-23 (`docs/specs/storeroom-json-crossing-proposal-review.md`; `docs/next-steps.md` · `N8`). The five gate-owned mechanics are settled here: `OM-2(b)`, `OM-4`, `OM-5`, `OM-6`, `OM-7(b)` (`JC-C7`). The store-side 17th status landed 2026-10-23, discharging `JC-C3`'s landing precondition. `JC-C6`'s residue dispositions are named and owned in `§6`, not absorbed. `[T]`

**Unit:** `U-STOREROOM-JSON-CROSSING`. **Prefix:** `D-GP-SRJC`. **Derivation:** `docs/specs/storeroom-json-crossing-proposal-review.md` · `decomposition` · `disposition = SPLIT-NOW` · `decider = the user` · `BUBBLE-ANSWERED: DECLINED | 2026-10-23`; one contract, both directions, no second unit (`A148` Q1). `[T]`

**Requirement of record:** `A147` (the ruling reaches both codecs; outside classes are refused at the package boundary), `A148` (the seven architect answers and the store-side widening), `A140` as amended (envelope and side-channel retired; `-0`, domain, walk discipline and store-surface clarification stand), and `A145` (the fourth dependency's form). `[A2 docs/decisions.md · A147 · A148 · A140 · A145]`

**Subject caption.** The contract governs the crossing's value tree — the JavaScript package's `encodeValue` walk, the Rust codec's `value_of` reader and `to_js_value` writer, and the JavaScript package's `decodeValue` decoder — for both directions of the `write`/`read` surface. It does not govern carrier addresses, tiers, op-ids, handles, file-tier JSON, or the native store's own API. `[T]`

## §1 — THE TREE, BOTH DIRECTIONS, ONE DEFINITION

**The admitted value tree is the bare JSON tree and nothing else.**

- A **string** is a sequence of Unicode scalar values (a JS string without a lone surrogate).
- A **number** is a finite IEEE-754 binary64 value; `-0` normalizes to `0` at the binding boundary (`A140`'s pin stands).
- A **boolean** is `true` or `false`.
- An **array** is a dense, genuine `Array` whose elements are admitted values. A hole, a subclass of `Array`, and an own accessor or own Symbol-keyed member on the array each refuse the whole value (`§2`).
- An **object** is a plain object (or a foreign-realm plain object) whose own enumerable string-keyed members carry admitted values. A class instance, any other prototype-carrying object, a `Proxy`, an own accessor, and an own Symbol-keyed member each refuse the whole value (`§2`). Member order is not semantically significant; array order is.
- **`null` is not admitted**, although JSON can spell it, because the store's own taxonomy makes `StoreValue::Null` an outside class. `undefined` is not admitted. No outside class is encoded as an object, a string, a marker, or a `{"v":…}` / `{"e":…}` envelope. `[A2 docs/decisions.md · A140 · A147 · A148 | the retired envelope; A1 src/value.rs:16 · :39 | the admitted/outside taxonomy]`

**⟶ AMENDED 2026-10-24 — THE CROSSING-DEFECT RULING PASS (`docs/defects.md` · `DEF-2026-10-24-C`; `RCA-8(g)`): THE PLAIN-PROTOTYPE PREDICATE IS DECLARED STRUCTURALLY — REALM-INDEPENDENT AND MUTATION-INDEPENDENT — BECAUSE THE SHIPPED PREDICATE COMPARED THE VALUE'S PROTOTYPE'S OWN-NAME CENSUS FOR EQUALITY AGAINST THIS REALM'S PROTOTYPES READ AT THE SAME CALL, AND SO REFUSED A LAWFUL FOREIGN-REALM PLAIN OBJECT AND A LAWFUL FOREIGN-REALM ARRAY IN EXACTLY THE STATES THE OBJECT AND ARRAY BULLETS ABOVE DECLARE ADMITTED.**
**The bullet list above stands byte-for-byte and is read THROUGH this one; the admitted tree, the refusals the bullets name, the one status and `§2`'s sixteen rows are unmoved. THIS clause decides ONE term only — what *"a plain object (or a foreign-realm plain object)"* means in the object bullet and what *"a dense, genuine `Array`"* means in the array bullet, that being the prototype test the walk takes through `protoClass` and `arrayClass`.**
**(1) THE OBJECT LIMB — a value is a PLAIN OBJECT when these three conditions hold, each read from the value and from ITS OWN prototype, and from no other realm's prototype by identity: (a) THE VALUE CARRIES A PROTOTYPE: `[[GetPrototypeOf]](value)` is an object.** (A `null`-prototype object is not this limb; it carries no prototype at all, and it is admitted through `§3`'s `p` classification `null`, unmoved by this clause.) **(b) THAT PROTOTYPE IS TERMINAL: `[[GetPrototypeOf]]([[GetPrototypeOf]](value))` is `null` — the value's prototype stands ONE link from the value and ends the chain. This condition is what refuses a CLASS INSTANCE**, whose prototype's own prototype is an `Object.prototype`: no census comparison is needed for it, and no census comparison may be substituted for it. **(c) THAT PROTOTYPE CARRIES THE STANDARD SET: the prototype's own member census CARRIES the ECMAScript standard `Object.prototype` member set — every name and every symbol of that set is an own member of the value's prototype — and any FURTHER own member that prototype carries is IRRELEVANT: it neither admits nor refuses.**
**(2) THE ARRAY LIMB — the same three conditions, with the standard `Array.prototype` member set and the standard chain, stated in full because the chain has one more link than the object's: the value's prototype carries the standard `Array.prototype` NAME set AND the standard `Array.prototype` SYMBOL-DESCRIPTION set (compared by name and by symbol DESCRIPTION, never by a symbol's identity, because `Symbol.iterator` in another realm is a different symbol value), and THAT prototype's own prototype is an `Object.prototype`-SHAPED prototype — a prototype which itself satisfies `(1)`'s (b) and (c). The standard chain is therefore `value → Array.prototype → Object.prototype → null` in EVERY realm, and the condition is stated on the chain and never on this realm's `Array.prototype` by identity, so a `vm` array, a worker's array and a `structuredClone`d array are admitted by the same rule as this realm's own. `Array.isArray` alone may not stand as the admission test and no `instanceof` test is taken.**
**(3) MUTATION-INDEPENDENCE, WHICH IS THE MEASURED DEFECT'S OWN TERM.** Adding ONE own property to THIS realm's `Object.prototype` or `Array.prototype`, or to the VALUE's realm's `Object.prototype` or `Array.prototype`, **LEAVES THE VERDICT UNCHANGED for a lawful value**: an injected own member is not a member of the standard set, so `(1)(c)` and `(2)` do not see it; and it stands on the value's own prototype or on its prototype's prototype without changing either prototype's terminality, so `(1)(b)` and `(2)`'s chain condition do not see it either. **The verdict therefore depends on the value's prototype's SHAPE, never on the two realms agreeing.** **THE DELETION OF A STANDARD MEMBER IS THE ONE MUTATION THAT IS NOT FROZEN, AND IT IS SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION: the reference set is the standard set INTERSECTED with this realm's live prototype census read per call (item `(4)`), so a deletion only removes a requirement and can never turn an admission into a refusal** — which is `C-11`'s fail-closed DoS in the other direction and equally forbidden.
**(4) THE REFERENCE SET IS READ PER CALL, AND `C-11` STANDS.** The value's prototype's own-name and own-symbol census is read AT THIS CALL from the live prototype and never from a module-level `const` taken at load (`plainProtoNames` `:708`, `arrayProtoNames` `:717`, `arrayProtoSymbols` `:721`, each keeping its per-call read), and the reference set is the ECMAScript standard member set intersected with that same per-call reading. **SO `C-11` IS PRESERVED IN ITS SUBSTANCE AND ONLY ITS INCIDENTAL TERM IS REMOVED: no live prototype census is ever frozen at load, a caller-side mutation cannot leave a stale reference set behind, and (item `(3)`) it cannot move a verdict either. `C-11` and this clause are therefore NOT in tension — this clause closes the same DoS and removes the equality against this realm that had turned the per-call read into a realm-agreement test.**
**(5) THE REMOVED TEST, NAMED AT ITS SYMBOL AND ITS MEASURED LINE, BECAUSE THE IMPLEMENTER IS OWED THE EXACT BYTES AND NOT A DESCRIPTION.** `bindings/js/index.mjs`'s `protoClass` (`:731`) refuses on a COUNT MISMATCH and then on a NAME-NOT-IN-THE-SET — `names.length !== plain.size` and `if (!plain.has(name)) return 'class'` at `:741`–`:742` — and `arrayClass` (`:766`) does the same for names and symbol descriptions at `:779`–`:781`. **THOSE TWO EQUALITY TESTS ARE WHAT THIS CLAUSE REMOVES; the census reads, the guarded `try` (`:735`–`:739` and `:771`–`:776`), the symbol-description comparison's realm-agnostic form and the rest of both bodies stand.** **AND THE IDENTITY COMPARISONS AT `:743` (`proto === Object.prototype ? 'plain' : 'foreign'`) AND `:782` (`proto === Array.prototype ? 'plain' : 'foreign'`) STAY — lawful on ONE reading only: they LABEL an admitted value's `p` classification (`§3`'s `plain` / `foreign` / `null`), and they decide NO admission. A verdict that turned on either of them would be the removed test under another name.**
**(6) WHAT STILL REFUSES, BYTE-FOR-BYTE AS THE BULLETS ABOVE AND `§2` DECLARE — this clause moves no refusal:** an own ACCESSOR refuses (`§2` row `15`, `accessorOrSymbolArm` `:748`); an own SYMBOL-KEYED MEMBER refuses (row `5`, the same helper); a CLASS INSTANCE refuses (row `14`, `(1)(b)` failing, its prototype's own prototype being an `Object.prototype`); a CLASS EXTENDING `Array` refuses (row `13`, `(2)` failing, its prototype's own prototype being an `Array.prototype` and not an `Object.prototype`-shaped one); a `Proxy` refuses (row `12`, `util.types.isProxy` at `:1163`); a SPARSE ARRAY and a hole refuse (row `16`, `:1179`). **AND EVERY ONE OF THEM REFUSES UNDER A PROTOTYPE MUTATION IN EITHER REALM TOO**, because their refusals rest on `(1)(b)`, on `(2)`'s chain condition or on the shape arms, none of which reads a count.
**(7) `JC-TC-07` IS MADE TRUE RATHER THAN MOVED.** `test-cases.md`'s `JC-TC-07` declares *"a foreign-realm plain object — admitted; the plain-object test is structural, not identity-based"*. It was FALSE at the shipped bytes, and `(1)`–`(3)` are what make it true: **the declaration stands in its own words and is discharged here, and the cell is re-derived on the same date only to NAME the mutation states the measurement produced (that part's own clause).**
**(8) THE MEASURED STATE THIS DISCHARGES, AND THE TWO LIMBS' HOMES NAMED RATHER THAN EDITED.** Measured 2026-10-24 through the package's own `write(addr, 'mem', value)`: one own property added to this realm's `Object.prototype` refused a lawful `vm` plain object as `a class instance or another prototype-carrying non-plain object`; one added to `Array.prototype` refused a lawful `vm` array as `a class extending Array`; a foreign realm mutated while this realm was pristine refused too; a NESTED `{outer: <vm plain object>}` refused; both realms mutated identically ADMITTED; and a same-realm `{a: 1}` under the same mutation ADMITTED. The censuses measured at the failing call were `12` own names on the foreign `Object.prototype` against `13` on this realm's, and `40` names / `2` symbols on the foreign `Array.prototype` against `41` / `2` on this realm's. **THOSE TWO FIGURES ARE THE MEASUREMENT AND NOT A RULE: the standard set is not pinned by a count here, because a clause that pinned one would fail again on the next standard method.**
**THE ARRAY LIMB'S HOME IS `docs/specs/storeroom-js/crossing.md §1.2` ROW `5` — *"a cross-realm array IS accepted (it is an `Array`), a class extending `Array` is not"* — AND THE OBJECT LIMB'S ARE THAT PART'S `§1.2` ROW `4` AND `§1.4` ITEM `4`'s structural plain-object test (a)–(g). ALL THREE STAND BYTE-FOR-BYTE: none of them was wrong, the predicate beneath them was, so they are named here as the clauses this one makes true and none is edited by this pass.**
**(9) NOTHING ELSE MOVES: no class, no status, no position, no figure, no `§2` row, no register row and no residue disposition is re-decided here, and `§2`'s sixteen-row census stands.** `[A1 bindings/js/index.mjs:708 · :717 · :721 · :731 · :735–:739 · :741 · :742 · :743 · :766 · :771–:776 · :779 · :780 · :781 · :782 · :748 · :787 · :795 · :1163 · :1174 · :1179 · :1194 · :1196 — the predicate, its two census reads, the two equality tests and every refusal arm they must leave standing, each opened at this pass's read | state or fail-state declaration; A2 docs/defects.md · `DEF-2026-10-24-C` | the finding this clause discharges; A2 docs/specs/storeroom-js/crossing.md §1.2 rows `4`/`5` · §1.4 item `4` | the two limbs' homes, unmoved | cross-reference]`

**⟶ AMENDED 2026-10-24 — THE GATE-11 RE-AUDIT PASS (read-only, `2026-10-24`; finding `F-RA-3`; the filed history `docs/defects.md` · `DEF-2026-10-24-C`; `RCA-8(g)`): ITEM `(3)`'S CLOSING SENTENCE IS WITHDRAWN AS AN ABSOLUTE AND NARROWED TO THE CALLER'S OWN REALM, BECAUSE THE REFERENCE SET IS THE STANDARD SET INTERSECTED WITH THIS REALM'S LIVE CENSUS AND A DELETION MADE IN ANOTHER REALM LEAVES A STANDARD NAME THE REFERENCE SET STILL REQUIRES.**
**The clause above — its nine items, its three conditions, the two equality tests it removes, the two identity comparisons' labelling duty, the two limbs' homes — stands byte-for-byte and is read THROUGH this one; the admitted tree, the refusals the bullets name, the one status and `§2`'s rows are unmoved. THIS clause re-decides ONE sentence of it.**
**(1) THE SENTENCE, QUOTED AT ITS BYTES AND SUPERSEDED AS AN ABSOLUTE: *"THE DELETION OF A STANDARD MEMBER IS THE ONE MUTATION THAT IS NOT FROZEN, AND IT IS SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION: the reference set is the standard set INTERSECTED with this realm's live prototype census read per call (item `(4)`), so a deletion only removes a requirement and can never turn an admission into a refusal"*. ITS SECOND CLAUSE — *"can never turn an admission into a refusal"* — IS FALSE OF THE REALM THE VALUE COMES FROM, for the reason item `(2)` reads in the bytes, and it is WITHDRAWN AS AN ABSOLUTE FROM THIS DATE.**
**(2) WHY, AT THE BYTES — A CODE READING TAKEN AT THIS PASS'S READ, NOT A MEASUREMENT:** `isStandardObjectPrototype` (`bindings/js/index.mjs:838`) requires **every** member of `referenceSet(OBJECT_PROTO_STANDARD_NAMES, plainProtoNames())` (`:847`) to be an own member of the value's prototype (`:848`), **and the reference set's live half is THIS realm's census alone** — `plainProtoNames` (`:792`, `Object.prototype`), `arrayProtoNames` (`:802`) and `arrayProtoSymbols` (`:806`, `Array.prototype`). **SO A DELETION MADE IN ANOTHER REALM IS NOT SEEN BY THE LIVE HALF AT ALL: the requirement stands, the value's own prototype no longer carries the name, and the carrying test fails.** Object limb: `protoClass` (`:874`) answers `'class'` at `:877` and the walk refuses the value as row `14` at `:1437`. Array limb: `arrayClass` (`:917`) answers `'class'` at `:929` (a throwing prototype read), at `:931` (the chain link) or at `:934`–`:935` (the carrying tests), and the walk refuses it as row `13` at `:1410`; **and a deletion from the VALUE's realm's `Object.prototype` reaches the array limb too, through `isStandardObjectPrototype(above)` at `:931`** — so that realm's arrays are refused as row `13` and its plain objects as row `14`.
**(3) THE NARROWED LAW, IN ITS OPERATIVE WORDS: a deletion from a prototype is safe — it removes a requirement and CAN NEVER TURN AN ADMISSION INTO A REFUSAL — IN THE CALLER'S OWN REALM, the realm whose `Object.prototype` and `Array.prototype` supply the reference set's live half (`:792` · `:802` · `:806`).** There, `C-11`'s fail-closed DoS cannot be reconstructed: the deleted name leaves the live half (`referenceSet` `:818`–`:821`) and with it the requirement, so every value of that realm is judged against what its own prototype still carries. **A deletion in a realm OTHER than the caller's is not that mutation at all — it mutilates the VALUE's own prototype — and the values of that realm are refused.**
**(4) THE DECLARED CONSEQUENCE, NAMED SO IT IS NOT FILED AS A DEFECT: A VALUE WHOSE OWN PROTOTYPE LACKS A STANDARD MEMBER IS REFUSED — as row `14` for the object limb and as row `13` for the array limb, each with ITS OWN ROW'S CLASS SPELLING (`a class instance or another prototype-carrying non-plain object` · `a class extending Array`), at its own value position.** **The ground is the predicate's own: the value's prototype is GENUINELY NO LONGER STANDARD-SHAPED — it lacks a member every standard `Object.prototype` / `Array.prototype` carries — and `§1`'s limbs refuse exactly that shape.** **The alternative is worse and is not taken:** admitting such a value would require the carrying test to accept a prototype that is not standard-shaped, which is the predicate's whole subject; **and the class answered is the ROW's, because rows `13` and `14` are the two arms whose subject is precisely *"the value's prototype failed `§1`'s limb"* (`§2`'s clause of this date, items `(1)`–`(2)`).**
**(5) WHAT THIS FORBIDS A CELL AND WHAT IT PERMITS, STATED SO NO PROPERTY IS WRITTEN FROM THE WITHDRAWN SENTENCE: a cell that drives a DELETION MUST NAME THE REALM IT DELETES IN.** A deletion in the **caller's** realm is asserted ADMISSION-PRESERVING — e.g. `delete Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty` in this realm leaves a same-realm `{ a: 1 }` ADMITTED, because the deleted name left the reference set with it. A deletion in the **value's** realm answers the value's own row at the value's own position — e.g. `vm.runInContext("delete Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty")` refuses a lawful `vm` plain object as row `14`, and `vm.runInContext("delete Array.prototype.map")` refuses a lawful `vm` array as row `13`, each position spelled as `parameter-semantics.md §2` spells it. **The two deletion drives are carried INSIDE `test-cases.md`'s `JC-TC-07` cell as its own states `(v)` and `(vi)` (that part's `§3` clause of this date); `JC-TC-07`'s twelve ADDITION drives and its control are unmoved. NO CELL MAY ASSERT THE WITHDRAWN ABSOLUTE, and no cell may assert that a deletion in EITHER realm leaves every verdict unchanged.**
**(6) `C-11` STANDS WHOLE, AND SO DOES THE FIRST HALF OF ITEM `(3)` ABOVE.** The per-call census read (`:792` · `:802` · `:806`) is unmoved; a caller-side ADDITION moves no verdict (item `(3)` of the clause above, unmoved); a caller-side DELETION moves no verdict either, which is item `(3)` here; no live census is ever frozen at load. **AND THE REALM-AGREEMENT TEST THE CLAUSE ABOVE REMOVES STAYS REMOVED: this clause restores NO equality against the foreign realm's prototype — a deletion in the value's realm is refused for that prototype's OWN missing member, not for a census divergence from this realm's — so a realm whose prototype carries a name THIS realm has deleted is ADMITTED.**
**(7) NO CODE CHANGE IS OWED BY THIS ITEM — the narrowing is a clause and not an edit — AND THE ONE CODE BYTE THAT PRINTS THE WITHDRAWN SENTENCE IS NAMED RATHER THAN QUIETLY LEFT BEHIND:** `referenceSet`'s doc block (`:810`–`:822`) carries *"a deletion can never turn an admission into a refusal"*, which is READ THROUGH item `(3)` as scoped to the caller's realm; **that comment byte is left to the pass that next edits the file and no behaviour byte moves with it.**
**(8) NOTHING ELSE MOVES: the three conditions, the two limbs' rows, the class spellings, the status, the positions and the register's `8` rows stand as filed; every code line this pass prints is measured at THIS pass's read, and the `bindings/js/index.mjs` entries of `§13`'s table have moved AGAIN since the clause of `2026-10-24` above re-pointed them — `§13`'s clause of this date re-points the ones this pass opened and names the ones it did not.** `[A1 bindings/js/index.mjs:792 · :802 · :806 · :810 · :818 · :821 · :838 · :847 · :848 · :874 · :877 · :917 · :931 · :934 · :935 · :1410 · :1437 — the two census readers, the reference set and its doc block, the carrying test, the two limbs' class decisions and their two refusal sites, each opened at this pass's read | state or fail-state declaration; A2 docs/defects.md · `DEF-2026-10-24-C` | the filed history this narrowing rests on; A2 docs/specs/storeroom-json-crossing/contract.md §2 — its clause of this date on rows `13`/`14` | the two rows this consequence is answered by | cross-reference]`

**Copy-in.** The package walks the caller's value first, classifies every reached value and member key, and emits the bare tree only if the whole value is admitted. The native half receives that tree through `napi`'s `serde-json` conversion and the codec's `value_of` reads a bare JSON value. A refusal is answered before any store verb is called. `[A1 bindings/js/index.mjs:1016 | encodeValue; bindings/js/src/codec.rs:332 | value_of]`

**Copy-out.** The store's value is written by the codec's `to_js_value` as the same bare tree, and the package's `decodeValue` returns that bare tree. No tag, class token, envelope, or side-channel appears in either direction. `[A1 bindings/js/src/codec.rs:857 | to_js_value; bindings/js/index.mjs:1801 | decodeValue]`

**One tree, two directions.** The same definition governs copy-in and copy-out; there is no split contract for copy-out and no second vocabulary (`A148` Q1). A tag or envelope that once carried a class is dead on this route; the only answer to an outside class is the refusal in `§2`. `[T]`

## §2 — THE SIXTEEN CLASSES, THEIR POSITIONS, AND THE ONE REFUSAL

**The marker count is nine; the class count is sixteen.** The old encoder's refusal arms spell `z` 1 · `u` 1 · `f` 1 · `y` 2 · `g` 1 · `F` 1 · `c` 4 · `P` 4 · `H` 1 = `16`; the contract pins the classes, not the markers (`A147`'s amendment). `[A2 docs/decisions.md · A147 | the label correction]`

| # | Class spelling (the package's exact reason text) | Failing position | Old marker | Answer |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `null` | value | `z` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 2 | `undefined` | value | `u` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 3 | `a function` | value | `f` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 4 | `a symbol` | value | `y` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 5 | `a value carrying a Symbol-keyed own member` | the containing value's own value position | `y` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 6 | `a bigint` | value | `g` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 7 | `a non-finite number` | value | `F` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 8 | `a value over the walk ceiling` | value | `c` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 9 | `a string carrying a lone surrogate` | value | `c` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 10 | `a string carrying a lone surrogate` | key | `c` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 11 | `a value that contains itself` | value | `c` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 12 | `a Proxy` | value | `P` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 13 | `a class extending Array` | value | `P` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 14 | `a class instance or another prototype-carrying non-plain object` | value | `P` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 15 | `a value carrying an own accessor member` | the containing value's own value position | `P` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 16 | `a sparse array` | value | `H` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |
| 17 | `an array carrying a non-index own member` | value of the containing array | `—` | `ValueRefusedAtBoundary` |

**Every row is refused at the package's walk, before any verb is called.** None reaches the store on the JavaScript route. None is coerced, repaired, dropped, or replaced. A refusal of a member refuses the whole containing value; a refusal of a member key likewise refuses the whole value and never drops the key. `[A2 docs/decisions.md · A147 · A148 Q3; A1 bindings/js/index.mjs:1016 | the walk's own class decisions]`

**⟶ AMENDED 2026-10-24 — THE PBT AUDIT PASS (`docs/defects.md` · `DEF-2026-10-24-B` · `F7`; `RCA-8(g)`): THE PRECEDENCE BETWEEN ROWS `3` AND `12` FOR A **CALLABLE** `Proxy` IS DECLARED — ROW `3` GOVERNS — BECAUSE THE TABLE ABOVE PLACES SUCH A VALUE IN BOTH ROWS AND DECLARED NO PRECEDENCE.**
**The table above and the clause above it stand byte-for-byte and are read THROUGH this one; no class spelling moves, no row is added or removed, and the sixteen-row census stands.**
**(1) THE ORDER THE WALK TAKES, WHICH IS THE LAW HERE: `bindings/js/index.mjs`'s walk tests `typeof value === 'function'` at `:1160` — row `3`'s arm — BEFORE its `util.types.isProxy` test at `:1163`, which is row `12`'s arm. A `Proxy` whose target is CALLABLE reads `typeof 'function'`, so it is refused in ROW `3`'s class `a function`; row `12`'s class `a Proxy` is answered for a `Proxy` that is NOT callable.**
**(2) THE TWO ROWS ARE THEREFORE NOT DISJOINT BY `typeof`, AND THE ROW A VALUE BELONGS TO IS THE ROW WHOSE TEST THE WALK REACHES FIRST — `null` → `undefined` → `string` → `boolean` → `bigint` → `number` → `function` → `symbol` → `Proxy` → ancestor → the `Array` class and shape → the plain-object class and shape (`:1138`–`:1198`). No cell may assert that a callable `Proxy` answers row `12`, and a property over these sixteen rows must take this precedence as declared rather than infer one.**
**(3) AND AT THIS ONE ARM THE CLASS AND PART ONE'S SUBJECT DIFFER: `valueTargetSpelling` (`:914`) renders a callable `Proxy` through its own `util.types.isProxy` branch (`:925`) to `classSpelling`, whose answer is `a Proxy` (`:811`) — so a callable `Proxy`'s CLASS is `a function` while its `<subject>` is `a Proxy` (`§2.1`'s clause of this date, its row `3` cell).**
**(4) NOTHING ELSE MOVES: row `12`'s spelling, the status, the positions and every other row's answer stand as filed; the register's own row-`4` spelling census is re-derived at `§8`'s clause of this date.** `[A1 bindings/js/index.mjs:1138 · :1140 · :1152 · :1153 · :1160 · :1161 · :1163 · :1169 · :1194 — the walk's test order; :914 · :925 · :806 · :811 | `valueTargetSpelling`'s `Proxy` branch and `classSpelling`, each opened at this pass's read | state or fail-state declaration; A2 docs/defects.md · `DEF-2026-10-24-B` · `F7` | the finding this clause discharges | requirement]`

**⟶ AMENDED 2026-10-24 — THE GATE-11 THIRD AUDIT PASS (read-only, `2026-10-24`; finding `F-RA-11`; `RCA-8(g)`): ROW `16`'S HOLE PREDICATE IS CHAIN-INCLUSIVE AT THE BYTES AND IS RE-DERIVED TO OWN-PROPERTY-ONLY, BECAUSE `i in value` CONSULTS `Array.prototype` AND SO ADMITS A SPARSE ARRAY CARRYING AN ELEMENT THE CALLER NEVER OWNED — A CODE CHANGE, OWED RED-FIRST AT `test-cases.md`'S `JC-TC-50`.**
**This section's table row `16` · `parameter-semantics.md §1` rows `1`, `3` and `4` · `§1`'s array bullet · `§4`'s *Read once* paragraph · this section's *"None is coerced, repaired, dropped, or replaced."* clause all stand byte-for-byte and are read THROUGH this one; no class spelling, position, marker, answer, row census, ceiling or status is re-decided here. THIS clause decides ONE term — what a HOLE is, and therefore what "dense" means — and states the code change that term owes.**
**(1) THE TERM, DECLARED: A HOLE IS AN INDEX BELOW `length` WITH NO **OWN** PROPERTY.** *"Dense"* means every index in `0…length − 1` is an own property of the array, and **a PROTOTYPE-CARRIED INDEX IS NOT AN ELEMENT of the array and never becomes one at the boundary.****
**(2) THE PREDICATE AT ITS BYTES, WHICH IS NOT THAT TERM: `bindings/js/index.mjs`'s `encodeValue` (`:1407`) tests its holes with `in` — `if (!(i in value)) return refusedAsClass(fault, value, 'a sparse array', path, { k: 'H' });` at `:1512`, inside the loop opened at `:1508` — and `in` is `[[HasProperty]]`, WHICH WALKS THE PROTOTYPE CHAIN. The element read at `:1546` is then `value[i]`, a `[[Get]]` that walks the same chain.**
**(3) THE STATE THE PREDICATE ADMITS, PRINTED AS THE COUNTEREXAMPLE IT IS: `Array.prototype[0] = 'x'; const a = new Array(2); a[1] = 'y'` — `a` owns index `1` only, its `length` is `2`, and the walk tests `0 in a` TRUE through the prototype, so ROW `16` DOES NOT FIRE and the crossing ADMITS; the tree that crosses is `['x', 'y']`, WHOSE INDEX `0` THE CALLER'S ARRAY NEVER OWNED. That is an element REPLACED at the boundary, and it falsifies BOTH `§1`'s *"a dense, genuine `Array`"* claim — `a` is not dense — AND this section's *"None is coerced, repaired, dropped, or replaced."* at its `replaced` limb, at a shape no clause of this contract names. (The third audit measured this state; this clause reads it from `:1512` and `:1546` at this pass's read.)**
**(4) AND WITH AN INHERITED ACCESSOR AT THE INDEX THE WALK RUNS CALLER CODE ON AN ADMISSION PATH: `Object.defineProperty(Array.prototype, '0', { get() { … } })` — `in` reads a presence bit and invokes nothing, so the hole test passes, and `value[0]` at `:1546` INVOKES THE GETTER, whose answer becomes the element. `§4`'s *"A getter cannot occur on an admitted value (an own accessor refuses the whole value, `§2` row 15), so 'read once' is a structural property of the admitted tree rather than a promise about a caller's getter"* is FALSE at this state, and it is declared TRUE FROM THE DATE THIS CLAUSE'S BYTE LANDS: an index carried only by the prototype is refused as row `16` and never read, and an own accessor at an index is refused as row `15` at `:1514`–`:1517`. `§4`'s paragraph needs no edit of its own and is read through this item.**
**(5) THE LAW, IN ITS OPERATIVE WORDS: AN ARRAY IS DENSE IFF EVERY INDEX IN `0…length − 1` IS AN OWN PROPERTY; an index whose only carrier is the prototype is a HOLE; and A PROTOTYPE-CARRIED INDEX — WITH OR WITHOUT AN ACCESSOR — IS THEREFORE NEVER READ.** So **(a)** a value whose array carries an index only through its prototype REFUSES as row `16`'s class `a sparse array`, AT THE ARRAY'S OWN VALUE POSITION, with the array's own class spelling as PART ONE's subject (`§2.1`'s six-arm clause of `2026-10-24`, unmoved) and the status `ValueRefusedAtBoundary`; **(b)** no inherited accessor at an index is read on ANY path — not on the admission path and not on a refusal path; **(c)** the refusal's SUBJECT is the class spelling and never the array's rendering, so the prototype's member does not reach the message either.**
**(6) THE BYTE FORM, NAMED SO THE IMPLEMENTER IS OWED THE EXACT READ: the test is `Object.hasOwn(value, i)` — the own-property read THIS FILE ALREADY USES AND ALREADY PREFERS (`:2144` · `:2179` · `:2261`, and the rule stated at `:2240`: *"`Object.hasOwn` and never `in`, which walks the prototype chain"*) — and NOT `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty`, which is a method taken from a mutable prototype (`:1122`'s own prohibition, and the deletion state `§1`'s clause of `2026-10-24` on `F-RA-3`, item `(3)`, declares admission-preserving).**
**(7) A CODE CHANGE IS OWED, AND IT IS OWED RED-FIRST: `test-cases.md`'s `JC-TC-50` is the cell — its expected answer is row `16`'s refusal at this shape with the inherited getter's counter at `0` — the red comes FIRST and the byte at `:1512` comes second (`implementation-plan.md §2` step `4`'s clause of this date, item `(1)`). UNTIL IT LANDS: no cell may assert this shape as an admission, no cell may assert an inherited element inside a crossed tree, and THE UNIT MAY NOT CLAIM `§1`'S DENSE TREE OR THIS SECTION'S *"NONE IS COERCED, REPAIRED, DROPPED, OR REPLACED"* AS AN ABSOLUTE AT THIS SHAPE.**
**(8) WHAT DOES NOT MOVE, NAMED SO THE FIX IS NOT SWEPT WIDER THAN ITS SUBJECT: row `17`'s predicate is UNAFFECTED, because it reads `Object.keys(value)` (`:1535`), which is OWN ENUMERABLE STRING-KEYED BY CONSTRUCTION; the array arm's ORDER (`:1503` → `:1512` → `:1514` → `:1535` → `:1541`), the position, the subject, the class spelling, the marker `H` and the status stand; and THE THREE `in` TESTS AT CARRIER POSITIONS — `:1867` (`encodeAddress`'s hole scan), `:2207` (`constraintAddress`'s) and `:3921` — ARE NOT THIS CLAUSE'S SUBJECT AT ALL: they read an ADDRESS, and this contract's subject caption (`§1`) excludes carrier addresses and their segment domain, so they are named here only so that no pass sweeps them in under this finding.**

**⟶ AMENDED 2026-10-24 — THE GATE-11 THIRD AUDIT PASS (read-only, `2026-10-24`; finding `F-RA-12`; `RCA-8(g)`): A `Proxy` STANDING AS A PROTOTYPE LINK IS ANSWERED AS `'class'` WITH **NO TRAP RUN**, TESTED WITH `util.types.isProxy` BEFORE ANY CENSUS READ, BECAUSE `protoClass` / `arrayClass` / `isStandardObjectPrototype` READ A LINK'S CENSUS AND A LYING TRAP CAN TURN A CLASS-SHAPED PROTOTYPE INTO AN ADMISSION — A CODE CHANGE, OWED RED-FIRST AT `test-cases.md`'S `JC-TC-51`.**
**The rows-`13`/`14` clause of `2026-10-24` above, `§1`'s clause of the same date and its `(1)`/`(2)` conditions, the `Proxy` value arm (`:1490`) and every class spelling stand byte-for-byte and are read THROUGH this one; no row is added, no census moves and no spelling changes. THIS clause decides ONE term of `§1`'s prototype predicate — WHETHER A PROTOTYPE LINK MAY BE READ AT ALL WHEN IT IS A `Proxy` — and states the code change it owes.**
**(1) THE BYTES, WHICH ARE THE DEFECT: the object limb reads a link's census through `protoClass` (`:886`) → `Object.getPrototypeOf(value)` (`:887`) → `isStandardObjectPrototype` (`:850`), whose `Object.getPrototypeOf(proto)` (`:854`) runs a `getPrototypeOf` trap and whose `Object.getOwnPropertyNames(proto)` (`:855`) runs `ownKeys` plus a `getOwnPropertyDescriptor` trap per key; the array limb reads the same census through `arrayClass` (`:929`) — `Object.getOwnPropertyNames(proto)` (`:937`), `Object.getOwnPropertySymbols(proto)` (`:944`), `Object.getPrototypeOf(proto)` (`:947`). TWO GUARDED `catch`es answer a trap that THROWS (`:856`–`:857`, `:948`–`:949`) — AND A `catch` CANNOT UN-RUN A TRAP THAT RETURNS.**
**(2) THE ADMISSION A LYING TRAP BUYS, PRINTED AS THE COUNTEREXAMPLE IT IS: `const lying = new Proxy(Object.create(null), { getPrototypeOf: () => null, ownKeys: () => Object.getOwnPropertyNames(Object.prototype), getOwnPropertyDescriptor: () => ({ value: 1, enumerable: true, configurable: true, writable: true }) }); const value = Object.create(lying);` — `isStandardObjectPrototype(lying)` answers TRUE (terminality at `:854`, the reference set's carrying test at `:859`–`:860`), `protoClass` answers `'foreign'` (`:889`), and the walk ADMITS the value at `:1551`/`:1555`. A PROTOTYPE SHAPED LIKE NOTHING AT ALL CROSSES AS A PLAIN OBJECT, AND THE CALLER'S TRAPS DECIDED IT.**
**(3) AND ON A REFUSAL PATH THE TRAPS RUN BEFORE THE REFUSAL IS ANSWERED: where a trap throws, the answer is `'class'` — row `14` at `:1555`, row `13` at `:1506` — but the trap has EXECUTED first, so `§2.1` item `(1)`'s headline *"NO USER CODE RUNS ON A REFUSAL PATH"* is FALSE IN THIS STATE, while item `(9)`'s narrower sentence — *"no user code the caller supplied as the refused value's conversion path runs"* — is literally TRUE, a class decision's census read being no conversion path. THAT IS THE WHOLE OF THE MISMATCH, AND IT IS WHY NO CELL CAUGHT IT: item `(1)` is a headline about every refusal path and item `(9)` counts conversion members only.**
**(4) THE COMMENT THAT SAID OTHERWISE IS NAMED AND IS FALSE FOR A LYING TRAP: `:1531`'s clause comment reads *"so a subclass, a sparse array, a `Proxy`-shaped prototype and a Symbol-carrying array each keep answering their own row"* — true only of a trap that REPORTS THE SHAPE THE `Proxy` STANDS FOR. ### §2.1 — THE MESSAGE'S PROVENANCE

**PART ONE is the store generator's own bytes.** `storeErrorMessage('ValueRefusedAtBoundary', args)` renders:

> `<subject> is not a value the JavaScript crossing admits, the crossing carries strings, booleans, finite numbers, plain objects and arrays`

where `<subject>` is `MessageArgs.segment` — the failing target's own spelling, rendered by the store's own target rule. The generator supplies the surrounding single quotes, so a `bigint` subject `10n` appears as `'10n'`; the package does not add a second quoting. **PART TWO is the package's and is appended beside it:**

> `, the value at <position> is <class>`

where `<position>` is the failing target's position spelling and `<class>` is one of the sixteen exact spellings in `§2`. **The package mints neither the status nor PART ONE; the store adds no `MessageArgs` member for the class or the position; the status's message has no second source.** `[A2 docs/decisions.md · A148 Q3; A1 src/error.rs:81 | storeErrorMessage; docs/specs/storeroom-native/contract.md §8.2 | the generator row and provenance clause]`

**⟶ AMENDED 2026-10-24 — THE CROSSING-DEFECT RULING PASS (`docs/defects.md` · `DEF-2026-10-24-D`; `RCA-8(g)`): NO USER CODE RUNS ON A REFUSAL PATH — THE SIX ARMS THAT RENDERED THE REFUSED VALUE NOW ANSWER THE CLASS SPELLING, BECAUSE `String(value)` INVOKED THE REFUSED VALUE'S OWN CONVERSION TRAP AND A GUARDED `catch` DOES NOT PREVENT AN INVOCATION.**

**⟶ AMENDED 2026-10-24 — THE CONVERSION-GUARD RESIDUAL PASS (the implementer's own residual report on `DEF-2026-10-24-D`'s item `(1)` landing; `RCA-8(g)`): ITEM `(1)`'S NO-USER-CODE RULE IS DECLARED AT ITS EXACT BOUNDARY — THE WALK STOPS AT A PROTOTYPE THE PACKAGE JUDGES LANGUAGE-STANDARD **BY SHAPE** — AND THE RESIDUAL THAT STOP LEAVES IS DECLARED PLAINLY, NAMED, SCOPED AND OWED, BECAUSE A CLAUSE THAT CLAIMED `(1)` UNIVERSAL WOULD BE FALSE AT THE BUILD AND A CLAUSE THAT SAID NOTHING WOULD LET A DONE ROW CLAIM IT.**
**The clause above — its six unconditional arms, its two conditional arms, its `String(value)` withdrawal, its guarded `catch`, the standard `Function.prototype.toString` machinery, `valueTargetSpelling`'s four substitutions, the targets column and the per-arm subject table — stands byte-for-byte and is read THROUGH this one; THIS clause decides ONE term of that clause, WHERE THE CONVERSION MEMBER IS SOUGHT AND WHERE THAT SEARCH ENDS, and no class, no status, no position, no message byte, no `§2` row and no register row is re-decided here.**

**(1) THE EXEMPTION'S EXACT BOUNDARY, AT ITS SYMBOLS AND THEIR MEASURED LINES.** `carriesConversionMember` (`bindings/js/index.mjs:1152`) answers "does this value carry a conversion member" by walking the refused value's prototype chain link by link (`while (link !== null)` at `:1157`, the next link taken at `:1161`), having first answered "no" for a primitive (`:1153`) and "yes" for a `Proxy` value or an own member on the value itself (`:1155`). **IT ASKS THREE QUESTIONS OF EACH LINK, IN THIS ORDER:** a `Proxy` LINK answers "carries" (`:1158`, and no trap runs); **`isLanguageStandardPrototype` (`:1138`) answers "DOES NOT CARRY" AND ENDS THE WALK AT THAT LINK** (`:1159`); otherwise `ownsConversionMember` (`:1125`) reads own DESCRIPTORS of `Symbol.toPrimitive`, `toString` and `valueOf` (`CONVERSION_MEMBER_KEYS` `:1119`, the descriptor read at `:1127`, so no getter is called) and answers "carries" if any of the three is own (`:1160`). **THE SECOND QUESTION RUNS BEFORE THE THIRD, AND THAT ORDER IS THE WHOLE OF THE EXEMPTION: a link the package judges language-standard by shape is never asked for its own conversion members at all.** `isLanguageStandardPrototype` answers true for an `Object.prototype`-shaped prototype — `isStandardObjectPrototype` (`:850`), its terminality test at `:854`, the link's own-name census at `:855` and the reference set's carrying test at `:859`–`:860` — and for an exotic array or a callable standing one link above such a prototype (`:1140`–`:1142`). **THE JUDGEMENT IS BY SHAPE AND BY NOTHING ELSE: no identity, no realm comparison and no count is taken, so ANY prototype which passes those tests is exempt — this realm's, another realm's, and one a caller synthesized to the same shape.** **AND A CHAIN THAT CANNOT BE READ ANSWERS "CARRIES"** (`:1164`–`:1168`), so an unreadable link costs the arm its rendering and runs nothing.

**(2) WHAT THE EXEMPTION BUYS, DECLARED BECAUSE IT IS THE DECLARED FORM AND NOT A LEAK.** The three sanctioned renderings stay reachable at the two conditional arms: an ordinary `{}` reaching `Object.prototype.toString` renders `'[object Object]'`, an array reaching `Array.prototype.toString` renders `'1,2'`, and a function reaching the standard `Function.prototype.toString` machinery renders its own source text — **each because the member it reaches is an own member of a shape-standard prototype and the walk stops at `:1159` BEFORE asking that prototype at `:1160`.** The rendering itself is `valueTargetSpelling`'s `String(value)` (`:1217`), reached after the conversion test at `:1216`.

**(3) THE RESIDUAL, PLAINLY AND IN THIS CONTRACT'S OWN VOICE: THE EXEMPTION IS BY SHAPE, SO IT ALSO EXEMPTS A CONVERSION MEMBER THAT IS NOT ECMASCRIPT'S OWN. A prototype the package judges language-standard by shape may carry an OVERRIDDEN `toString` or `valueOf` — or an ADDED `Symbol.toPrimitive`, which is no own member of `Object.prototype` at all — and the carrying test at `:859`–`:860` asks only that the reference set's NAMES be present, never that a member's VALUE be the intrinsic function. WHERE THE REFUSED VALUE'S ONLY CONVERSION MEMBER SITS ON SUCH A PROTOTYPE, THE ARM RENDERS THE VALUE AND THAT MEMBER RUNS: the package may not cache a pristine prototype identity (`C-11`), so it cannot tell an intrinsic from an override by any read it is allowed to make, and the rendering is the one place where code the caller supplied is reached after the guard has passed.** **THE ARMS ARE THE TWO CONDITIONAL ONES AND NO OTHERS — row `3` (`:1487`, subject `String(fn)`) and row `8`'s DEPTH arm (`:1462`–`:1463`, subject `String(value)`) — these being the only two arms whose PART ONE subject is a rendering of an object or a function (`:1101`–`:1106`).**

**(4) THE RESIDUAL'S SCOPE, CORRECTED FROM THE BYTES AND NOT TAKEN FROM THE REPORT: IT IS NEITHER FOREIGN-REALM-ONLY NOR ROW-`8`-ONLY.** **(a) REALM:** item `(1)`'s judgement takes no realm comparison, so an override made in the CALLER's own realm is exempt exactly as another realm's is, and the hole is symmetric in the realm — **a clause that declared it a foreign-realm hole would be narrower than the build.** **(b) ARMS:** row `3` is exposed by the same order, because a function's chain reaches `Function.prototype` first and `isLanguageStandardPrototype` answers true there on the shape test alone (`:1140`–`:1142`), so an overridden `Function.prototype.toString` is exempt and `String(fn)` runs it. **(c) MEMBERS:** the exempt member need not be an override; an ADDED `Symbol.toPrimitive` on a shape-standard prototype is exempt too, because an own member beyond the standard set is irrelevant to the carrying test (`§1`'s `(1)(c)` clause of 2026-10-24, unmoved). **(d) THE ARMS THAT ARE NOT EXPOSED, named so no cell hunts for them:** the six re-derived arms — rows `5`, `11`, `13`, `14`, `15`, `16` — and row `17` answer `fault.subject` and never render (`refusedAsClass` `:1390`, the choice at `valueReject` `:1261` · `:1264`); row `8`'s NODE arm hands the class spelling STRING (`:1426`) and rows `1`, `2`, `4`, `6`, `7` hand primitives (`:1465`–`:1485`); row `9` answers its class spelling above the test (`:1210`); row `10` hands the value argument's name (`:1580`); and row `12`'s `Proxy` is answered by `classSpelling` above the test (`:1203`).

**(5) THE SECOND LIMB, WHICH ITEM `(1)`'S ENUMERATION DOES NOT REACH AND WHICH AN IDENTITY READ COULD NOT CLOSE — DECLARED AS A CODE-READING CLAIM: THE SANCTIONED RENDERING IS ITSELF LANGUAGE MACHINERY, AND THAT MACHINERY MAKES FURTHER READS OF ITS OWN WHEN IT RUNS.** `Object.prototype.toString` reads `@@toStringTag` off the refused value, so **an own `Symbol.toStringTag` ACCESSOR is invoked at row `8`'s depth arm** — a value the walk refused on ENTRY (`:1462`–`:1463`), before the shape arm which would have refused an own accessor (`:1558`); and `Array.prototype.toString` calls `join`, which converts EACH ELEMENT, so **the refused array's ELEMENTS' conversion members run** — elements the walk never classified, the element walk lying BELOW the arm (`:1541`). **Item `(1)`'s letter names the refused value's own three members and those reached through its chain, and neither of these is either; so this limb is NAMED AND NOT CLAIMED CLOSED, no cell drives it, and no pass may read item `(3)` as the whole of the residual.**

**(6) THE REMEDY THAT WAS REJECTED, AND WHY IT IS A DECISION AND NOT A CLAUSE: IDENTITY AGAINST THE INTRINSIC MEMBERS** — capturing `Object.prototype.toString`, `Array.prototype.toString` and `Function.prototype.toString` (or a pristine prototype) at load and comparing each exempt link's member against the captured function. **IT WOULD CLOSE ITEM `(3)` AND WOULD LEAVE ITEM `(5)` STANDING**, because the standard `join` IS the intrinsic and it is the elements' members that run. **THE REJECTION'S GROUND IS `C-11`'S NO-FROZEN-PROTOTYPE-FACT RULE, WHICH THIS CONTRACT ALREADY CARRIES (`§1`'s clause of 2026-10-24, item `(4)`, with its per-call census reads `plainProtoNames` `:792` · `arrayProtoNames` `:802` · `arrayProtoSymbols` `:815`): a pristine snapshot taken at load is precisely the frozen prototype fact `C-11` forbids, so identity has to RE-OPEN `C-11` FIRST — and re-opening it is the architect's decision, not a clause of this contract.** **AND THE OBVIOUS ORDERING SWAP IS NOT A REMEDY EITHER, SAID SO NO PASS TAKES IT AS ONE: asking a shape-standard link for its own conversion members BEFORE stopping (swapping `:1159` against `:1160`) answers "carries" for every ordinary `{}` and every array — `Object.prototype.toString` IS an own member of it — so the two conditional arms would answer their class spelling UNCONDITIONALLY and item `(2)`'s three sanctioned renderings would be withdrawn.**

**(7) THE ONE CLAUSE-LEVEL ALTERNATIVE, NAMED AND NOT TAKEN: WITHDRAWING THE WITH BRANCH AT THE TWO CONDITIONAL ARMS** — re-deriving item `(4)` of the clause above so rows `3` and `8`'s depth arm answer their class spelling UNCONDITIONALLY — **closes items `(3)` and `(5)` at both arms with no identity read and no `C-11` re-opening. It is not taken here because it withdraws renderings the clause above declares and cells carry, and because no measurement and no filing demands it; it is what a future pass takes if identity is refused.**

**(8) THE REVISIT CONDITION, IN A FORM A FUTURE PASS CAN ACT ON: THIS DECLARATION IS RE-OPENED BY THE FIRST OF THESE TO BE FILED, AND BY NOTHING ELSE** — **(a)** an architect ruling re-opening `C-11` so that a pristine prototype identity may be captured; **(b)** an architect ruling that the two conditional arms answer their class spelling unconditionally, i.e. item `(7)`; **(c)** a cell or a measurement that DRIVES a refused value at row `3` or at row `8`'s depth arm whose only conversion member sits on a shape-standard prototype **and whose member RUNS**. **WHICHEVER IS FILED, THE PASS THAT TAKES IT OWNS: this clause; item `(4)`'s two `<subject>` cells; whichever cells assert those two arms' WITH-branch renderings, where such cells are filed; and the two code sites — the test at `:1216`, and the arms at `:1487` and `:1462`–`:1463`.** **AND `DEF-2026-10-24-D`'S OWN DISCHARGE IS UNMOVED BY THIS REVISIT: the finding's item `(1)` is bounded here, not withdrawn, and no new defect ID is filed by this clause.**

**(9) WHAT THE UNIT CLAIMS AND WHAT IT DOES NOT, SO A DONE ROW CANNOT OVERCLAIM.** **IT CLAIMS ITEM `(1)` FOR: (a) a conversion member OWN on the refused value (`:1155`); (b) a conversion member owned by a prototype the walk does NOT judge language-standard — a class's own prototype, a custom prototype, a `Proxy` link (`:1158` · `:1160`) — answered as the row's class spelling with no rendering reached; and (c) the six re-derived arms and row `17`, which render nothing at all.** **IT DOES NOT CLAIM ITEM `(1)` FOR: (d) a conversion member owned by a prototype judged language-standard by shape, at rows `3` and `8`'s depth arm (item `(3)`); nor (e) the further reads item `(5)` names.** **THE UNIT'S OWN SENTENCE IS THEREFORE THE ONE THAT FOLLOWS, AND IT IS THE ONE A DONE ROW READS: *no user code the caller supplied as the refused value's conversion path runs on a refusal path, except where that path lies on a prototype judged language-standard by shape at the two conditional arms, and except the further reads of the sanctioned rendering itself.***

**(10) NO CELL DRIVES IT, AND NO CODE CHANGE IS OWED BY THIS CLAUSE.** This is a declaration of the boundary the landed code HAS, and not a defect ruling: no measurement produces a refusal with the wrong class, position, subject or status, and the unit's four files measured at this pass name neither an overridden nor an added conversion member on a shape-standard prototype — no cell of `test-cases.md` drives such a state, and none is owed until item `(8)` is filed. **A CELL MAY NOT ASSERT THAT THE EXEMPTION'S HOLE DOES NOT EXIST, AND NO CELL MAY ASSERT AN "OVERRIDDEN STANDARD PROTOTYPE" STATE AS CLOSED.** No status, no gate state, no `§2` row and no register row moves; the red set's census and the green run's figures are unmoved.

**(11) THE WORD "OWN" — IN THE PER-ARM SUBJECT TABLE'S ROW-`3` CELL AND DEPTH-ARM CELL ABOVE, AND IN ITEMS `(4)` AND `(5)` OF THE CLAUSE ABOVE — IS READ THROUGH ITEM `(1)` OF THIS CLAUSE, WHICH IS THE BUILD: *"own conversion member"* reads *"a conversion member the refused value CARRIES — own on it, or owned by a prototype its chain reaches before the exemption stops the walk"*, which is `carriesConversionMember`'s answer at `:1216`.** **This is a READING and not a re-derivation: the arms, the renderings, the class spellings and every cell stand exactly as they stand, and a property written from those cells reads them this way.**

**(12) EVERY `bindings/js/index.mjs` FIGURE THIS CLAUSE PRINTS IS MEASURED AT THIS PASS'S READ AND IS THE FIGURE FOR ITS SYMBOL FROM THIS DATE; the `§13` re-pointing clauses are read THROUGH this item for those symbols, because two line sets for one symbol would be a contradiction rather than a stale figure (`§13`'s own words).** **MEASURED HERE, AND THEREFORE SUPERSEDING THE FIGURE PRINTED THERE:** `carriesConversionMember` `:1152` · `ownsConversionMember` `:1125` · `isLanguageStandardPrototype` `:1138` · `isStandardObjectPrototype` `:850` (printed `:838`) · `referenceSet` `:830` (printed `:818`) · `arrayProtoSymbols` `:815` (printed `:806`) · `accessorOrSymbolArm` `:894` (printed `:882`) · `isAdmittedArray` `:961` (printed `:941`) · `isPlainObjectValue` `:969` (printed `:949`) · `valueTargetSpelling` `:1192` (printed `:1109`) · `valueReject` `:1261` · `valuePosition` `:1065` (printed `:1045`) · `VALUE_ARGUMENT_POSITION` `:1063` (printed `:1043`) · `refusedValue` `:1375` (printed `:1292`) · `refusedAsClass` `:1390` (printed `:1307`) · `encodeValue` `:1407` (printed `:1311`) · `protoClass` `:886` (printed `:874`) · `arrayClass` `:929` (printed `:917`) · `ENCODER_CEILING` `:1350` with `NODE_CEILING` `:1351` (printed `:1267`/`:1268`) · the node-ceiling arm's `refusedValue` call `:1426` with its test at `:1425` · the `Array`-subclass arm `:1506` with its test at `:1503` (printed `:1410`) · the member-key arm `:1580` with its `hasLoneSurrogate(name)` test at `:1574` (printed `:1462` — WHICH ON THIS TREE IS THE DEPTH CEILING'S `if` AND NOTHING ELSE) · `decodeValue` `:2277` (printed `:2159`), opened here though outside this clause's subject. **AND THE TWO THAT RESOLVE AS PRINTED, re-opened at the same read: `plainProtoNames` `:792` ✓ and `arrayProtoNames` `:802` ✓. THE SAME SUPERSESSION APPLIES TO THE SAME FAMILY'S FIGURES IN `§4`'S CLAUSE OF 2026-10-24 ON `F-RA-4`, measured at this read: `:1268` → `:1351`, `:1328` → `:1424`, `:1329` → `:1425`, `:1396` → `:1492`, `:1401` → `:1497`, `:1428` → `:1546`, `:1464` → `:1582`.** `[A1 bindings/js/index.mjs:712 · :732 · :774 · :1119 · :1125 · :1127 · :792 · :802 · :815 · :830 · :832 · :850 · :854 · :855 · :859 · :860 · :1138 · :1139 · :1140 · :1141 · :1142 · :1152 · :1153 · :1155 · :1156 · :1157 · :1158 · :1159 · :1160 · :1161 · :1163 · :1164 · :1168 · :1203 · :1210 · :1216 · :1217 · :1218 · :1261 · :1264 · :1375 · :1390 · :1407 · :1424 · :1425 · :1426 · :1462 · :1463 · :1465 · :1485 · :1487 · :1492 · :1497 · :1503 · :1506 · :1541 · :1546 · :1558 · :1574 · :1580 · :1582 · :2277 — the conversion-member test with its three questions and their order, the two shape tests and the prototypes they exempt, the two conditional arms' rendering site, the six class-subject arms, the element and member walks the depth arm precedes, and every line the supersession of item `(12)` re-points, each opened at this pass's read | state or fail-state declaration; A2 docs/defects.md · `DEF-2026-10-24-D` | the finding whose item `(1)` this clause bounds, and the residual report it answers | requirement; A2 docs/specs/storeroom-json-crossing/contract.md §1 — its clause of `2026-10-24`, item `(4)` | `C-11`'s no-frozen-prototype-fact rule, which the rejected remedy would re-open first | cross-reference]`

**⟶ AMENDED 2026-10-24 — THE GATE-11 THIRD AUDIT PASS (read-only, `2026-10-24`; findings `F-RA-13` and `F-RA-12`; `RCA-8(g)`): THE CONVERSION-GUARD RESIDUAL CLAUSE ABOVE IS RE-DERIVED — THREE OF THE STATES IT PICTURED AS CLOSEABLE-NOWHERE ARE REFUSABLE WITH DESCRIPTOR-ONLY, PER-CALL READS AND ARE DECLARED OWED AS CODE, RED-FIRST — AND THE RESIDUAL IS NARROWED TO THE ONE STATE THAT GENUINELY NEEDS A PRISTINE IDENTITY: AN **OVERRIDDEN** `toString` OR `valueOf` ON A SHAPE-STANDARD PROTOTYPE.**
**That clause — its twelve items — stands byte-for-byte and is read THROUGH this one, EXCEPT the items this clause names as re-derived at their own numbers; this clause's item `(4)` re-derives item `(9)`'s claim sentence, item `(5)` re-derives item `(8)`'s triggers, item `(3)` re-derives item `(5)` and item `(10)`, item `(7)` re-derives item `(11)`'s reading of *"own"* for ONE key, and item `(2)` narrows item `(3)` and confines item `(6)`. `§2.1` item `(1)`'s rule, item `(2)`'s three sanctioned renderings, the six unconditional arms, the two conditional arms' own sites (row `3` at `:1487`, row `8`'s depth arm at `:1462`–`:1463`), every class spelling, position, subject, status, `§2` row and register row are UNMOVED, and NO ADMISSION IS CHANGED BY ANY ITEM HERE — every remedy below is a READ of a descriptor, and every state it refuses is a state whose intended rendering would run caller code or would answer a caller's own data.**
**(1) THE THREE STATES ITEM `(3)` OF THE CLAUSE ABOVE PICTURED AS CLOSEABLE-NOWHERE, EACH NOW DECLARED REFUSABLE, WITH ITS EXACT REMEDY — DESCRIPTOR-ONLY, PER-CALL, WITH NO PRISTINE-PROTOTYPE IDENTITY AND NO FROZEN PROTOTYPE FACT, SO `C-11` IS UNTOUCHED AND ITEM `(6)`'S REJECTION DOES NOT REACH THEM:**
**(1a) AN **ADDED** `Symbol.toPrimitive` ON A SHAPE-STANDARD PROTOTYPE.** The exemption's carrying test asks NAMES only — `isStandardObjectPrototype` (`:850`), the reference set at `:859` and the carrying loop at `:860`, drawn from `OBJECT_PROTO_STANDARD_NAMES` (`:712`–`:725`), **WHICH HOLDS NO SYMBOL AT ALL** (the block at `:710`–`:711` declares that `Object.prototype` carries no own symbol member), and from `ARRAY_PROTO_STANDARD_SYMBOL_DESCRIPTIONS` (`:774`), which holds `Symbol.iterator` and `Symbol.unscopables` and nothing else — so an ADDED `Symbol.toPrimitive` leaves the shape test TRUE, the stop at `:1159` exempts the link, and `String(value)` at `:1217` REACHES IT, ToPrimitive's `@@toPrimitive` lookup being a `[[Get]]` that traverses the chain. **THE REMEDY: ONE OWN-DESCRIPTOR READ OF `Symbol.toPrimitive` PER LINK, TAKEN BEFORE THE EXEMPTION STOPS THE WALK.** **IT SEPARATES AN ADDITION FROM AN INTRINSIC EXACTLY, because NO realm's `Object.prototype`, `Array.prototype` or `Function.prototype` carries an own `Symbol.toPrimitive`; and it leaves item `(2)`'s three sanctioned renderings intact, `'[object Object]'`, `'1,2'` and a function's source text being `toString`'s answers and never `@@toPrimitive`'s.** **AND IT IS NOT ITEM `(6)`'S REJECTED ORDERING SWAP, SAID PLAINLY SO NO FUTURE READER RE-REJECTS IT BY ASSOCIATION: the swap asks ALL THREE conversion keys of every shape-standard link and so answers "carries" for every ordinary `{}` and every array; THIS ASKS ONE KEY and answers "does not carry" for every intrinsic prototype.**
**(1b) AN OWN **OR ADDED** `Symbol.toStringTag` ACCESSOR.** `Object.prototype.toString` reads `@@toStringTag` off the refused value — a `[[Get]]` that traverses the chain — so an own `@@toStringTag` accessor IS INVOKED at row `8`'s depth arm, the walk having refused the value on ENTRY at `:1462`–`:1463` above the shape arms (`:1558`), and an ADDED one on a shape-standard prototype is reached through the chain the same way. **AND NO ADMISSION MOVES, WHICH IS WHY A DESCRIPTOR READ IS THE WHOLE REMEDY: a value carrying an own `Symbol`-keyed member is already REFUSED at its own position — row `5`'s spelling `a value carrying a Symbol-keyed own member`, through `accessorOrSymbolArm`'s SYMBOL half at `:899`, this pass's `§2` clause on rows `15`/`5` stating that a symbol-keyed ACCESSOR is row `5`'s and not row `15`'s — and at row `8`'s depth arm it is refused as row `8` regardless.** **THE REMEDY: ONE OWN-DESCRIPTOR READ OF `Symbol.toStringTag` AT THE VALUE AND AT EACH LINK THE RENDERING WOULD TRAVERSE, TAKEN BEFORE THE RENDERING; WHERE A DESCRIPTOR EXISTS THE ARM ANSWERS ITS CLASS SPELLING.** **THE READ IS TAKEN FOR THE KEY AND NOT MERELY FOR THE ACCESSOR BIT, DELIBERATELY: a DATA `@@toStringTag` member would move the SUBJECT (`String({ [Symbol.toStringTag]: 'X' })` is `'[object X]'`), and a subject that depends on a caller's data is not the declared form; the accessor case is the no-run case and the data case is the determinism case, and ONE read answers both.** **No realm's `Object.prototype` or `Array.prototype` carries an own `Symbol.toStringTag` either, so the read answers "absent" for every intrinsic chain and item `(2)`'s renderings stand.**
**(1c) THE ARRAY-ELEMENT LIMB — item `(5)`'s SECOND HALF.** `Array.prototype.toString` (a standard NAME, `:772`) calls `join`, which takes each element by `[[Get]]` and converts it — so at row `8`'s DEPTH arm, where the refused value is an ARRAY, THE ELEMENTS' CONVERSION MEMBERS RUN, and the walk never classified those elements, the element loop (`:1541`) lying BELOW the arm. **THE REMEDY, AND IT INTRODUCES NO FOURTH RULE: (i) EVERY OWN INDEX PROPERTY IN `0…length − 1` IS READ BY **DESCRIPTOR**, so an own ACCESSOR at an index answers the class spelling AND ITS GETTER NEVER RUNS; and (ii) EVERY DATA ELEMENT IS SUBJECTED TO THE SAME RENDER GUARD THIS CLAUSE PUTS ON THE REFUSED VALUE — item `(1a)`'s `Symbol.toPrimitive` descriptor read taken at the element and at each link below the language's own, item `(1b)`'s `Symbol.toStringTag` descriptor read taken at the element and at each traversed link, and `carriesConversionMember` (`:1152`, descriptor-only by its own construction, per-call, a `Proxy` link answered "carries" at `:1158` with no trap run).** **AN ELEMENT'S `ToString` IS A RENDERING AS THE VALUE'S `String` IS, SO ONE GUARD GOVERNS BOTH AND A TEST THAT ASKED ONLY `carriesConversionMember` WOULD LEAVE AN ADDED `Symbol.toPrimitive` ON A STANDARD PROTOTYPE RUNNING FOR AN OBJECT ELEMENT.** **WHERE THE GUARD ANSWERS "CALLER CODE IS REACHABLE", THE ARM ANSWERS ITS CLASS SPELLING; WHERE EVERY ELEMENT IS A PRIMITIVE — or an object whose conversion and tag members sit only on an intrinsic prototype — THE ARM RENDERS `'1,2'`, AND THAT SANCTIONED RENDERING STANDS AND IS THE CONTROL OF ITS OWN CELL.**
**(2) THE RESIDUAL, NARROWED TO WHAT GENUINELY NEEDS IDENTITY: AN **OVERRIDDEN** `toString` OR `valueOf` ON A SHAPE-STANDARD PROTOTYPE — a NAME the reference set requires whose VALUE is not ECMAScript's intrinsic function (and, at an array's `toString`, an overridden `join`, which `Array.prototype.toString` calls). THE GROUND IS `C-11` AND IT IS RESTATED HERE AND ONLY HERE, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT OF THE NARROWING: `isStandardObjectPrototype`'s carrying test (`:859`–`:860`) asks WHETHER THE NAME IS OWN, never WHETHER THE MEMBER'S VALUE IS THE INTRINSIC, and NO READ THIS CONTRACT ALLOWS DISTINGUISHES AN INTRINSIC FROM AN OVERRIDE — so closing THIS state requires a pristine prototype or intrinsic captured at load, WHICH IS THE FROZEN PROTOTYPE FACT `C-11` FORBIDS. Item `(6)`'s rejection therefore STANDS FOR THIS STATE ALONE, `C-11` has to be re-opened by the architect before the identity remedy can be taken, and item `(3)` of the clause above is re-read from this date as naming THIS state and not the three of item `(1)`.**
**(3) THE THREE STATES OF ITEM `(1)` ARE DECLARED REFUSABLE AND ARE OWED AS CODE, RED-FIRST, AND ARE NOT THE RESIDUAL FROM THIS DATE: their cells are `test-cases.md`'s `JC-TC-52` (1a), `JC-TC-53` (1b) and `JC-TC-54` (1c); each red comes FIRST and its byte SECOND (`implementation-plan.md §2` step `4`'s clause of this date, item `(3)`).** **AND ITEM `(5)` OF THE CLAUSE ABOVE IS SUPERSEDED IN ITS *"NAMED AND NOT CLAIMED CLOSED"* HALF: both of its limbs are now DECLARED CLOSABLE, NAMED WITH THEIR REMEDIES AND OWED; its NAMING of them stands, and its *"no cell drives it"* stands as the record of the pass that wrote it.** **AND ITEM `(10)`'S *"NO CELL DRIVES IT, AND NO CODE CHANGE IS OWED BY THIS CLAUSE"* IS SUPERSEDED IN BOTH HALVES FOR THE THREE STATES AND STANDS FOR THE OVERRIDE CASE: cells ARE owed and named, code IS owed; the override case keeps no code owed until item `(5)`'s trigger `(c)` is filed.** **UNTIL THE THREE LAND, ITEM `(1)`'S RULE IS STILL FALSE AT THEM, AND THE UNIT MUST SAY SO — item `(4)`.**
**(4) ITEM `(9)`'S CLAIM SENTENCE IS RE-DERIVED SO A DONE ROW CANNOT OVERCLAIM, AND IT NAMES THREE EXCLUSIONS RATHER THAN TWO.** **THE UNIT'S SENTENCE FROM THIS DATE READS: *no user code the caller supplied as the refused value's conversion path runs on a refusal path, except (i) where that path lies on a prototype judged language-standard by shape at the two conditional arms AND IS AN OVERRIDDEN `toString` or `valueOf`; (ii) at the three states item `(1)` declares refusable, until their code lands; and (iii) at a `Proxy` standing as a prototype link, until the two guards of `§2`'s clause of this date on `F-RA-12` land.*** **ITEM `(9)`'S SUB-ITEMS `(a)`, `(b)`, `(c)` AND `(d)` STAND; ITS SUB-ITEM `(e)` — *"the further reads item `(5)` names"* — IS RE-DERIVED AS *"the three owed states of item `(1)` here"*, and the sentence's limit is therefore the OVERRIDE case plus the three owed states plus the `Proxy`-prototype limb, and NOTHING ELSE.**

**(b) THE GUARD'S ANSWER IS NOT THE DEFECT AND IS NOT WITHDRAWN BY THIS ITEM: from this date NO USER CODE RUNS AT A CARRIER POSITION'S SUBJECT RENDERING — the guard answers for the address as it answers for a refused value, so an own `toString`/`valueOf`/`@@toPrimitive` at that slot is not invoked and the message is rendered from the package's own facts. What is defective is the ANSWER GIVEN WHERE THE GUARD TRIPS: a position that declares no row class answered `undefined`, which is neither a class spelling, nor a rendering, nor a declared descriptor of anything.**
**(c) THE LAW, IN ONE SENTENCE, AND EVERY CARRIER-POSITION CLAUSE OF THIS UNIT READS IT FROM THIS DATE: *at a carrier position the subject is THAT POSITION'S OWN DECLARED DESCRIPTOR, NEVER `undefined`.*** **A subject is a fact about the POSITION and not a renderer's willingness to render, so a position whose rendering may not run answers its own class half, and a position with no class half answers its rendering and takes the guard that rendering needs.**
**(d) THE DESCRIPTORS, SLOT BY SLOT — the address-bearing slots are enumerated so no carrier position is left to inference, each measured at this pass's read.**
**(d)(i) THE `targetSpelling` (`:1020`) SLOTS, WHICH TAKE NO ROW CLASS AT ALL AND SO CANNOT ANSWER `undefined`:** the op-id slot `:2189` (`encodeOpId` `:2180`); the verb's first-argument (instance) slot `:2578` (`stateOf` `:2573`); the handle slots `:3141` (`listen`), `:3514` and `:3522` (`removeListener`), `:4119` and `:4127` (`registerOn` `:4100`); the address-set and set-member slots `:3153`, `:3165` and `:3173` (`listen`); and the `register` listener-argument slot `:4105` (`registerOn`). **THEIR DESCRIPTOR IS `docs/specs/storeroom-js/surface.md §2.5` item 5 (a)'s own value rendering — the value's `String` (`:1035`), with a `bigint`'s `` n `` (`:1026`) and a `Proxy`'s class spelling (`:1034`) — and a rendering that throws answers `classSpelling(value)` (`:1036`–`:1037`).**
**(d)(ii) THE ONE `valueTargetSpelling` (`:1305`) CARRIER SLOT:** `constraintAddress`'s slot `:2334`, reached from `constraintEntry` `:2307` through `encodeConstraints` `:2294`. **ITS DESCRIPTOR IS `valueTargetSpelling`'s WITHOUT branch — the address's own rendering, single-quoted by the carrier's own spelling — where `renderGuardTrips` (`:1278`) does not trip, and `classSpelling(value)` (`:995`–`:1006`) where it does.** **A row class is NOT that slot's descriptor: the constraint list's refusal is a CARRIER refusal and `§2`'s seventeen rows are the WALK's, which is why the slot passes no class and why the fallback below is what it answers.**
**(d)(iii) THE `cfg.listeners` ENTRY SLOT, WHICH CONSULTS NO RENDERER AT ALL AND IS NAMED FOR COMPLETENESS: `encodeListeners`'s entry refusal (`:2267`–`:2272`) passes the subject as the literal `listeners` and carries the class half as `classSpelling(entry)` (`:2271`) inside its prose, while the entry's `address` crosses through `encodeAddress` (`:2280`) and answers wherever that carrier answers. ITS DESCRIPTOR IS THEREFORE A LITERAL PLUS A CLASS SPELLING — two forms that cannot answer `undefined` — and item `(a)`'s regression is NOT reachable at it.**
**(e) THE FALLBACK WHERE A ROW CLASS IS NOT SUPPLIED, STATED AS THE HELPER'S OWN TERM AND NOT AS A CALLER'S DUTY: `classSpelling(value)`.** **The guarded `catch` at `:1335`–`:1338` already answers exactly that for a rendering that throws, so the two branches agree from this date and `valueTargetSpelling` has ONE fallback rather than two.** **Its form is the parameter's default or the guard's own null-check — `rowClass` defaulting to the value's class spelling, or `return rowClass === undefined ? classSpelling(value) : rowClass;` — and the two spellings are the same law: NO CALL SITE CAN PRINT `undefined`, and a carrier site that forgets the argument prints the class spelling instead of garbage.**
**(f) A CODE CHANGE IS OWED, RED-FIRST, AND IT IS THE GUARD'S RETURN PLUS THE PARAMETER'S FALLBACK: the red cell is `test-cases.md`'s `JC-TC-55` — the construction of item `(a)`, asserting the carrier slot's subject as the address's CLASS SPELLING `a plain object` with the address's own `toString` counter at `0` — and the byte comes second (`implementation-plan.md §2` step `4`'s clause of this date, arm `(vi)`).** **The cell asserts the SUBJECT byte and no status: the carrier refusal's status, code and prose are that route's own and this item re-decides none of them.** **Until the byte lands, NO CELL MAY ASSERT A SUBJECT AT A CARRIER POSITION WHOSE ADDRESS CARRIES A CONVERSION OR TAG MEMBER, and no DONE row may claim item `(1)`'s rule at that slot.**
**(g) WHAT DOES NOT MOVE:** the value arm's two-argument call at `:1381`, `valueTargetSpelling`'s four named substitutions (`:1307` · `:1308` · `:1316`–`:1317` · `:1323`), `String(value)` at `:1334`, the guarded `catch`, the generator's single quoting, PART TWO, the status, every `§2` row and every per-arm subject cell. **AND A RENDERING THE GUARD DOES NOT TRIP IS BYTE-IDENTICAL AT EVERY SLOT: a primitive address, an address of plain `{}` shape (`String({})` is `'[object Object]'` and no limb answers for it), and an in-domain segment list all print exactly what they printed before the guard existed.**

**(2) `F-RA-19` — THE RENDER GUARD'S CONDITION, STATED PER ARM, BECAUSE THE ELEMENT LIMB CANNOT ANSWER AT ROW `3` AND THE TAG LIMB IS CONSERVATIVE AT BOTH ARMS.**
**(a) THE ITEM RE-DERIVED, AND ITS RESTATED GROUND: item `(6)` of the `F-RA-13` clause above says the WITH branch is taken where the render guard's three tests do not all answer "no caller code", WHICH IS TRUE OF THE CODE AND INCOMPLETE AS A LETTER — it does not say WHICH test can answer at WHICH arm, so a reader cannot predict the subject byte at either.** **`renderGuardTrips` (`bindings/js/index.mjs:1278`) is ONE conjunction of three (`:1279` · `:1280` · `:1281`) applied IDENTICALLY at both conditional arms (the single guard call at `:1333`), so THE CODE'S CONDITION IS: a conversion member is carried, OR a `@@toStringTag` member is carried, OR the value is an array whose element reads trip. Item `(6)`'s ground is therefore RESTATED IN THE CODE'S OWN WORDS AS *"a conversion or tag member is carried"*, the element limb being the third disjunct and the one that needs an array; the per-arm readings follow.**
**(b) ROW `3`'S ARM, PREDICTED EXACTLY: the refused value is a FUNCTION. `carriesConversionMember` (`:1182`) can answer at it — an own `Symbol.toPrimitive`/`toString`/`valueOf` (`:1185`), one on a link the exemption does not reach (`:1197`), the added `@@toPrimitive` on a shape-standard link (`:1191`), a `Proxy` link (`:1189`) — and `carriesTagMember` (`:1224`) can answer at it (`:1228`, the link loop `:1229`–`:1234`); `arrayElementsReachCallerCode` (`:1252`) CANNOT, because a function is not an array (`:1253` · `:1256`).** **SO THE SUBJECT AT ROW `3` IS: the function's own source text where NEITHER the conversion limb NOR the tag limb answers; `'a function'` where EITHER answers; `'a Proxy'` for a callable `Proxy` (the earlier arm at `:1316`–`:1317`); and `classSpelling(value)` where the rendering throws (`:1338`).**
**(c) THE TAG LIMB IS CONSERVATIVE AT ROW `3`, NAMED AND NOT HIDDEN: `Function.prototype.toString` READS NO `@@toStringTag`, so a refused function carrying ONLY an own or an added `@@toStringTag` takes the WITH branch and answers `'a function'` although NO CALLER CODE WAS REACHABLE — the arm's answer is the declared fallback's own family and no admission and no run is at stake, but the letter must not promise the source text for that state.** **The alternative, making the tag limb ask which rendering the arm takes, is named at item `(e)` and NOT taken.**
**(d) ROW `8`'S DEPTH ARM, PREDICTED EXACTLY, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME LIST: the refused value is whatever stands at depth `127` (`:1579`–`:1580`), so the three limbs answer BY SHAPE.** **(i) A PRIMITIVE LEAF: no limb answers (`:1183` · `:1225` · `:1253`) and the value's own `String` rendering stands (`'0'`, `'NaN'`).** **(ii) AN ORDINARY OBJECT LEAF: the conversion limb and the tag limb both answer, and they are the rendering's own read paths — `Object.prototype.toString`'s `@@toStringTag` `[[Get]]` and `ToPrimitive`'s `@@toPrimitive` lookup — while the element limb does not; so the class spelling stands where either answers and the object's own rendering (`'[object Object]'`) where neither does.** **(iii) AN ARRAY LEAF: the conversion limb, the tag limb AND the element limb (`:1252`–`:1270`) all answer; the class spelling stands where ANY answers and the array's own join (`'1,2'`-family) where none does; AND THE TAG LIMB IS CONSERVATIVE AT THIS SHAPE TOO, `Array.prototype.toString` and `join` reading no `@@toStringTag` on the array ITSELF.** **(iv) A FUNCTION LEAF: as row `3`'s arm, the element limb answering nothing.**
**(e) THE ALTERNATIVE, NAMED AND NOT TAKEN: making the tag limb RENDERING-PATH-AWARE — asking the tag only where `Object.prototype.toString` is the rendering actually reached, i.e. at an ordinary object or at an object ELEMENT of an array — would restore the source text for a tagged function and the array's own join for a tagged array. It is a CODE CHANGE to `renderGuardTrips` (`:1278`) and it is NOT taken here: no admission, no run and no status moves, the class spelling is a lawful subject at both arms, and a filing which names a tagged function's withdrawn source text as a defect takes it.**
**(f) THE TWO CELLS RE-DERIVED IN PLACE, AND WHY A CELL FORM IS USED: a per-arm condition cannot reach the place a reader PREDICTS a subject, which is the per-arm table, so ROW `3`'s `<subject>` cell GAINS THE TAG MEMBER TO ITS WITH LIST and ROW `8`'s DEPTH-arm `<subject>` cell GAINS IT TOO, each naming item `(2)` of this clause as its reading; NO OTHER CELL OF THAT TABLE MOVES, and the cells' WITHOUT-branch renderings, targets and positions are byte-identical.**
**(g) NO CODE IS OWED BY THIS FINDING, AND THE LEDGER SAYS SO: the condition declared here IS the code's (`:1279`–`:1281` at the one guard call `:1333`); what was missing was the letter's per-arm content and the two cells' WITH lists.**

**(3) `F-RA-20` — THE ELEMENT LIMB'S REPEAT IS RULED TO BE A PATH TEST AND NOT A VISITED SET: AN ANCESTOR-STACK SEMANTICS IS THE LAW, AN ALIASED ELEMENT DOES NOT TRIP THE GUARD, AND THE CODE CHANGE IS OWED.**
**(a) THE STATE, AT ITS BYTES: `arrayElementsReachCallerCode` (`:1252`) carries a `seen` set — it answers "caller code" where `seen.has(value)` (`:1257`) and records each array it enters (`seen.add(value)`, `:1258`), and nothing is ever released — so with `e = [1]` and the refused value `arrayChain([e, e], 127)` THE SECOND OCCURRENCE OF `e` AT AN INDEX ANSWERS `true` at `:1257` and row `8`'s depth arm answers its CLASS SPELLING where item `(1c)`'s own ground — *"where EVERY ELEMENT IS A PRIMITIVE … no guard is tripped and the sanctioned `'1,2'` renders"* — and the array's sanctioned `'1,1'` say the WITHOUT branch.** **`seen` IS THE ONE TERM OF ITEM `(1c)` NO CLAUSE DECLARES: the item states the descriptor read (`:1261`), the accessor answer (`:1263`) and the element guard (`:1264`), and says nothing about repetition.**
**(b) THE RULING, IN ONE SENTENCE: THE ELEMENT LIMB TESTS THE CURRENT PATH AND NOT THE VISITED SET — a sub-value that recurs ON THE PATH the walk is currently reading trips the guard, and a sub-value THE SAME PARENT HOLDS TWICE does not, because rendering it twice runs the same code twice and reaches no member the first read did not.** **THE GROUND IS THE UNIT'S OWN AND IT IS ALREADY DECLARED TWICE: `§4`'s clause of 2026-10-24 on `F-RA-4` states that the walk *"carries an ANCESTOR STACK and never a visited set"*, which is what admits an acyclic diamond, and `test-cases.md`'s `JC-TC-11` admits `const a = {}; ({x: a, y: a})` in the words *"the ancestor stack refuses cycles, not shared ancestry"*.** **AN ALIAS IS NEITHER A CYCLE NOR CALLER CODE: this limb's declared question is whether an element's conversion path is REACHABLE, and an aliased element's answer is the answer its first occurrence gave.**
**(c) THE WITNESS THE RULING MUST SURVIVE, WHICH IS THE CYCLE: a self-containing array (`const a = []; a.push(a)`) must still trip, or the limb recurses without bound. The figures are `:1252` · `:1257` · `:1258` · `:1264` · `:1278`; the descriptor reads (`:1261`–`:1263`), the `try` (`:1254`–`:1268`) and every answer of the limb stand.** **RED-FIRST: the red cell is `test-cases.md`'s `JC-TC-56`; the byte comes second (`implementation-plan.md §2` step `4`'s clause of this date, arm `(vii)`).**
**(e) WHAT A CELL MAY AND MAY NOT ASSERT FROM THIS DATE: MAY — an aliased element at row `8`'s depth arm renders the array's own join (`'1,1'` for `[e, e]` with a primitive-bearing `e`) with every element counter at `0`, and a self-containing array at that arm answers that row's class spelling. MAY NOT — that a repeat at TWO POSITIONS trips the guard, or any assertion built from the visited-set reading.**
**(f) THE ALTERNATIVE, NAMED AND REFUSED: declaring that an aliased element answers the class spelling — the build's answer today, whose only available ground would be that a repeated array cannot be told from a cycle — is REFUSED because THE BUILD CAN TELL THEM APART (the path is in the recursion's own arguments, `:1264`), and because that alternative withdraws a sanctioned rendering item `(1c)` declares while naming no caller code — the over-refusal this contract forbids in the other direction, which `referenceSet`'s own comment states for a DELETION at `:828`–`:829` (*"a deletion can never turn an admission into a refusal — `C-11`'s fail-closed DoS in the other direction, equally forbidden"*) and which applies here with the shapes reversed: a test may not refuse a state whose declared answer is a rendering.**

**(4) `F-RA-21` — THE ARM CENSUS AND THE NON-EMPTINESS TERM HAVE ONE OPERATIVE HOME AND THIS CLAUSE POINTS AT IT FROM THE PER-ARM HOME.**
**(a) THE STACK, AT ITS BYTES: THE PER-ARM HOME PRINTS THREE DIFFERENT LAWS AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS OPERATIVE.** **(i) `F2`'s clause of 2026-10-24 (the clause that introduced the per-arm table) item `(3)(a)` reads *"the CLASS SPELLING is PART ONE's subject at exactly THREE arms"*.** **(ii) The `DEF-2026-10-24-D` clause's item `(5)(a)` re-derives it to NINE arms and its item `(5)(b)` adds *"`<subject>` is NON-EMPTY at every arm of `§2` from this date"*.** **(iii) `§2`'s clause of 2026-10-24 on `F-RA-5`, item `(5a)`, re-derives the count as TEN arms UNCONDITIONALLY — row `8`'s NODE arm, rows `5`, `9`, `11`, `12`, `13`, `14`, `15`, `16` and row `17` — plus TWO CONDITIONAL ones (row `3` and row `8`'s depth arm), and states that `<subject>` is NOT guaranteed non-empty because the depth arm renders the refused value and an EMPTY ARRAY renders `''`.** **THE POINTER HAS RUN ONE WAY ONLY: `§2` says its reading is the operative one and re-derived "here and nowhere else", while the per-arm home still prints (i) and (ii).**
**(b) THE POINTER, IN ONE SENTENCE AND IN THIS SECTION BECAUSE THE PER-ARM TABLE LIVES HERE: `§2`'s item `(5a)` IS THE OPERATIVE READING of the arm census and of the non-emptiness term from this date; `F2`'s item `(3)(a)` ("THREE arms") and the `DEF-2026-10-24-D` clause's items `(5)(a)` ("NINE arms") and `(5)(b)` are its SUPERSEDED DRAFTS, and neither may be quoted, cited or built from.** **The two conditional arms' CONDITION is the one item `(2)` of this clause states, so a reader reaching the table reads the census at `§2` and the condition here, and nowhere else.**
**(c) THE NON-EMPTINESS TERM CORRECTED, WITH THE WITNESS THE OPERATIVE READING ALREADY NAMES: item `(5)(b)`'s *"`<subject>` is NON-EMPTY at every arm of `§2` from this date"* IS FALSE AT THE BUILD AND IS WITHDRAWN IN THAT HALF.** **A refused value of `[]` at depth `127` — `arrayChain([], 127)`, the depth arm's own state at `:1579`–`:1580` — renders `''`: `renderGuardTrips` (`:1278`) answers "no caller code" for an empty array (`:1252`–`:1266`, the element loop at `:1260` never entered, and no conversion or tag member on the chain) and `String([])` at `:1334` is the empty string. `<subject>` IS THEREFORE NOT NON-EMPTY AT EVERY ARM and a cell may not assert that it is.** **The clause's OTHER half stands: `<subject>` is still NOT guaranteed to be a class spelling.**
**(d) WHY THE OLD HOME'S OWN REASON NO LONGER CARRIES THE TERM: item `(5)(b)`'s support was that *"the empty subject an empty `Array` subclass answered at row `13` is retired with that cell"* — a real retirement (row `13`'s cell is the class spelling, that clause's item `(2)`) which removes ONE empty subject and leaves the DEPTH arm's, which the same clause's item `(4)` created by keeping the value's own rendering there.
